(3.24)

We introduced the symbolic notation

\mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.76213pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.60124pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.76213pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.60124pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.76283pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=7.61539pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.23784pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=6.5654pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\mathtt{e}^{s}\,\mathtt{e}^{r}=-{\mathrm{i}}\,\hbar\,{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}\,\mathsf{H}\,(\mathtt{e}^{s}\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{r})={\mathrm{i}}\,\hbar\,(2\pi)^{d}\,\frac{\delta(s+r)}{s^{2}-m^{2}}\ .

(3.25)

Integrating over 10 out of 12 momenta in (
3.24
) and resolving the delta-functions, this term finally results in

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tilde{G}_{2}^{\star}(p_{1},p_{2})_{\text{\tiny 1PI}}^{{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}}\big|_{\rm partial}\\[4.0pt]
&\quad=({\mathrm{i}}\,\hbar)^{3}\,\Big(-\frac{\lambda}{3!}\Big)^{4}\,\frac{3}{5}\,\frac{3}{4}\,\frac{3}{3}\,\frac{3}{2}\,\frac{(2\pi)^{d}\,\delta(p_{1}+p_{2})}{(p_{1}^{2}-m^{2})\,(p_{2}^{2}-m^{2})}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\int_{l_{1},l_{2}}\,\frac{1}{\big(l_{1}^{2}-m^{2}\big)\,\big(l_{2}^{2}-m^{2}\big)\,\big((l_{1}+l_{2})^{2}-m^{2}\big)\,\big((p_{1}+l_{1})^{2}-m^{2}\big)\,\big((p_{2}+l_{2})^{2}-m^{2}\big)}\ .\end{split}

(3.26)

This partial contribution to the two-point function at two-loops in braided
\lambda\,\phi^{3}
-theory corresponds to the diagram

p_{1}
p_{2}
l_{1}
l_{2}
p_{1}{+}l_{1}
p_{2}{+}l_{2}
l_{1}{+}l_{2}

Again all noncommutative corrections vanish and the result is the same as in the commutative case.

4
Braided Schwinger–Dyson equations

In ordinary quantum field theories, the Schwinger–Dyson equations are consistency equations satisfied by full correlation functions which represent the quantum analogues of the classical equations of motion.
They are most readily derived in the path integral formalism with integration measure that is invariant under infinitesimal variations of the fields.
As our braided quantum field theories do not admit a path integral formulation, we will show how such consistency relations arise from the purely algebraic perspective of homological perturbation theory.
We adapt arguments originating in
[
16
,
17
,
18
,
20
]
for quantum
A_{\infty}
-algebras to the setting of braided
L_{\infty}
-algebras. Our main goal is to better understand to what extent noncommutative corrections enter the correlation functions of braided scalar field theory through a detailed derivation and analysis of the braided analogues of the Schwinger–Dyson equations.

4.1
Schwinger–Dyson equations as a recursion relation

Consider a braided scalar field theory in the setting of Section
2
.
Decomposing the perturbed projection as
{\tilde{\mathsf{P}}}=\mathsf{P}+\mathsf{P}_{{{{\bm{\delta}}}}}
, the braided homological perturbation lemma implies

\begin{split}{\tilde{\mathsf{P}}}&=\mathsf{P}\,\Big(\mathrm{id}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])}+\big(\mathrm{id}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])}-{{{\bm{\delta}}}}\,\mathsf{H}\big)^{-1}\,{{{\bm{\delta}}}}\,\mathsf{H}\Big)=\mathsf{P}\,\big(\mathrm{id}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])}-{{{\bm{\delta}}}}\,\mathsf{H}\big)^{-1}\ .\end{split}

It follows that the full projector
{\tilde{\mathsf{P}}}
can be computed through the recursion relation (as also hinted at in
[
11
]
)

\begin{split}{\tilde{\mathsf{P}}}=\mathsf{P}+{\tilde{\mathsf{P}}}\,{{{\bm{\delta}}}}\,\mathsf{H}\ .\end{split}

(4.1)

Acting on a basis of
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])
of symmetric degree
n>0
, the free projector
\mathsf{P}
vanishes by the trivial projection onto
\mathbbm{R}
in (
2.10
), giving

{\tilde{\mathsf{P}}}={\tilde{\mathsf{P}}}\,{{{\bm{\delta}}}}\,\mathsf{H}\ .

(4.2)

We show that this identity implies a braided version of the Schwinger–Dyson equations for the braided BV theory.

As previously, we consider a basis for
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])
in cohomological degree
0
, built from antifields
\phi_{i}^{+}\in V_{2}
, which in momentum space are the fields
\mathtt{e}^{p_{i}}
.
We evaluate the recursion (
4.2
) on

\begin{split}\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{n}}\ \in\ \mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])_{0}\ .\end{split}

(4.3)

The left-hand side is simply the all-orders correlation function

\begin{split}\mathsf{P}_{{\bm{\delta}}}(\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{n}})&={\tilde{G}}^{\star}_{n}(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})\ .\end{split}

(4.4)

The right-hand side of (
4.2
) requires a choice of deformation
{{{\bm{\delta}}}}
.

4.2
Free theory and the braided Wick theorem

To derive the Schwinger–Dyson equations for the free braided scalar field theory, we use the deformation

\displaystyle{\bm{\delta}}_{0}=-\mathrm{i}\,\hbar\,\mathsf{\Delta}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}\ .

Because
\mathsf{\Delta}_{{\textrm{\tiny BV}}}
has symmetric degree
-2
and we use the trivial projection
\mathsf{P}
, correlation functions in the free theory are non-zero only for even numbers of field insertions. We use (
4.2
) to show that they encode the braided Wick theorem proposed in
[
25
]
.

Using (
4.4
) and (
4.2
), the free
2n
-point is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{G}_{2n}^{\star 0}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2n}):=\mathsf{P}_{{\bm{\delta}}_{0}}(\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{2n}})=\tilde{\mathsf{P}}\,(-{\mathrm{i}}\,\hbar\,{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}\,\mathsf{H})\,(\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{2n}})\ .

Using (
2.5
), (
2.9
), (
2.11
) and (
A.4
) we obtain the expansion

\displaystyle\begin{split}&-{\mathrm{i}}\,\hbar\,{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}\,\mathsf{H}\,(\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{2n}})\\[4.0pt]
&\hskip 56.9055pt=\frac{1}{2n}\,\sum_{i\neq j}\,\mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=40.13052pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=40.13052pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.98808pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=34.20238pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.68811pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=31.14409pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{i}\,\mathsf{R}_{\alpha_{i+1}}\cdots\mathsf{R}_{\alpha_{j-1}}(\phi_{j})\ \mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{i-1}}\\
&\hskip 128.0374pt\odot_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha_{i+1}}(\mathtt{e}^{p_{i+1}})\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha_{j-1}}(\mathtt{e}^{p_{j-1}})\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{j+1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{2n}}\ ,\end{split}

(4.5)

for
n>1
, with the free two-point functions given by

\tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2}(p_{i},p_{j})=-{\mathrm{i}}\,\hbar\,{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}\,\mathsf{H}\,(\mathtt{e}^{p_{i}}\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{j}})={\mathrm{i}}\,\hbar\,\frac{(2\pi)^{d}\,\delta(p_{i}+p_{j})}{p_{i}^{2}-m^{2}}=:\mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.98808pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.26018pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.68811pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=7.66022pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{i}\,\phi_{j}=\mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 5.08595pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 5.08595pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.2721pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.26018pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.9721pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=7.66022pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{j}\,\phi_{i}\ ,

(4.6)

as in (
3.3
). The absence of braiding phase factors in the final equality is a consequence of momentum conservation.

Using (
2.14
) to unravel the actions of the inverse
{\mathcal{R}}
-matrices, and applying
\tilde{\mathsf{P}}=\mathsf{P}+\mathsf{P}_{{\bm{\delta}}_{0}}
to the remaining products of antifields in (
4.5
), we arrive at the recursion relations

\displaystyle\tilde{G}_{2n}^{\star 0}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2n})=\frac{1}{2n}\,\sum_{i\neq j}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{\,{\mathrm{i}}\,p_{j}\cdot\theta\,(p_{i+1}+\cdots+p_{j})}\ \mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.98808pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.26018pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.68811pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=7.66022pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{i}\,\phi_{j}\ \tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n-2}(p_{1},\dots,\widehat{p_{i}},\dots,\widehat{p_{j}},\dots,p_{2n})\ ,

(4.7)

for
n\geqslant 1
, where we define
\tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{0}:=1
. The right-hand side is a sum of
2n\,(2n-1)
terms involving
2n-2
-point correlation functions,
where the hat notation means to omit the corresponding entry. For
n=1
the formula (
4.7
) reproduces the two-point function (
4.6
).
This is our form of the Schwinger–Dyson equations for the free braided scalar field theory.

Let us compare (
4.7
) with the standard textbook form of the Schwinger–Dyson equation in the commutative case, which is also derived in
[
16
,
17
]
from the perspective of
A_{\infty}
-algebras. We multiply through (
4.7
) by
p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}
to find

\displaystyle(p_{1}^{2}-m^{2})\,\tilde{G}_{2n}^{\star 0}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2n})=\frac{{\mathrm{i}}\,\hbar}{2n}\,\sum_{j=2}^{n}\,(2\pi)^{d}\ \delta(p_{1}+p_{j})\ {\mathrm{e}}^{\,{\mathrm{i}}\,p_{j}\cdot\theta\,(p_{2}+\cdots+p_{j})}\ \tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n-2}(p_{2},\dots,\widehat{p_{j}},\dots,p_{2n})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{1}{2n}\,\sum_{i=2}^{n}\ \sum_{j\neq i}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{\,{\mathrm{i}}\,p_{j}\cdot\theta\,(p_{i+1}+\cdots+p_{j})}\ \mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.98808pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.26018pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.68811pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=7.66022pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{i}\,\phi_{j}\ (p_{1}^{2}-m^{2})\,\tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n-2}(p_{1},\dots,\widehat{p_{i}},\dots,\widehat{p_{j}},\dots,p_{2n})\ .

Up to the overall factor
\frac{1}{2n}
, the first line coincides with the standard form of the Schwinger–Dyson equations, with the right-hand side representing a deformation of the usual contact terms with phase factors respecting the cyclic ordering of field insertions.

The meaning of the remaining terms from the second line is that, rather than singling out a specific field insertion (here
\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}
), the (braided)
L_{\infty}
-algebra formulation averages the usual Schwinger–Dyson equations over all permutations of the field insertions, leaving an expression that is manifestly (braided) symmetric. This is in contrast to the
A_{\infty}
-algebra formulation of
[
16
,
17
]
, where the brackets do not have any symmetry properties like the
L_{\infty}
-brackets do. Indeed the classical algebra of observables for an
A_{\infty}
-structure is the unsymmetrized tensor algebra, and correspondingly the fattening of maps used in the homological perturbation lemma to compute correlators differ from those of an
L_{\infty}
-structure precisely by the symmetrization used in the latter
[
29
]
. From an algebraic perspective, our form (
4.7
) of the Schwinger–Dyson equations is much more elegant as it highlights the symmetries of the correlation functions, while it can be brought into the standard form (without the factor
\frac{1}{2n}
) by a combinatorial argument, as we discuss further below.

4.2.1
Examples

We can illustrate the formula (
4.7
) using the diagrammatic calculus of Section
3.3
. In the following a large blue filled circle with
n
emanating black lines represents the full
n
-point correlation function
\tilde{G}_{n}^{\star}(p_{1},\dots,p_{n})
.

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{P}}\,{\bm{\delta}}_{0}\,\mathsf{H}\,(\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{2}}\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{3}}\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{4}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{{\bm{\delta}}_{0}}\,{\bm{\delta}}_{0}\,\mathsf{H}\,(\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{2}}\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{3}}\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{4}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}\,({\bm{\delta}}_{0}\,\mathsf{H})^{2}\,(\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{2}}\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{3}}\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{4}})

Compared to the right-hand side of (
4.7
), the 12 terms have combined to three terms and cancelled the overall factor of
\frac{1}{4}
.

Putting everything together, we arrive at

\displaystyle{\footnotesize\hbox to74.15pt{\vbox to61.3pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 37.07529pt\lower-30.65198pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

}}{
}

(4.9)

This is the braided Schwinger–Dyson equation for the four-point function in the free braided scalar field theory, which is just the braided Wick theorem in this case
[
25
]
.

Example 4.10

(
Six-point function).

One can compute an explicit form for the six-point function from the Schwinger–Dyson equation (
4.7
) for
n=3
by substituting the four-point functions (
4.9
) into the 30 terms appearing on the right-hand side; this is used in the calculations of Section
3.6
. The final result is the braided Wick theorem for six antifields in momentum space proposed by
[
25
]
:

(4.11)

4.2.2
Braided Wick expansion

We will now extablish that, generally, iterating (
4.7
) reproduces the braided Wick theorem, adapted to momentum space from
[
25
]
, which determines the free
2n
-point function as

\begin{split}\tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2n})=\frac{1}{n!\,2^{n}}\ \sum_{\sigma\in S_{2n}}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{{\mathrm{i}}}{2}\,\sum\limits_{i<j}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{j}}\ \prod_{k=1}^{n}\,\mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 16.2146pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=29.0889pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 16.2146pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=29.0889pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 13.91963pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=24.95729pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 13.61966pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=24.35733pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{\sigma(2k-1)}\,\phi_{\sigma(2k)}\ ,\end{split}

(4.12)

where
S_{2n}
is the symmetric group of degree
2n
.
The momentum-dependent phase factor implicitly depends on the permutations, since for each
\sigma\in S_{2n}
it is accompanied by a product of delta-functions
\delta(p_{\sigma(2k-1)}+p_{\sigma(2k)})
from the individual two-point functions (
4.6
) for
k=1,\dots,n
, which trivialises many terms. This expresses the braiding that arises in going from ordering
1\cdots 2n
to ordering
\sigma(1)\cdots\sigma(2n)
of the field insertions.

The general formula (
4.12
) was written down in
[
25
]
using heuristic arguments, and checked explicitly for
n=2
using the braided version of the homological perturbation lemma. Indeed, we have already checked the formula (
4.12
) for
n=1,2,3
in Section
4.2.1
using the Schwinger–Dyson equations (
4.7
). We can now provide a rigorous proof of the braided Wick expansion by showing that the recursive equation (
4.7
) has solution of the form (
4.12
).

Starting from (
4.12
), we observe that the sum over products of contractions can be rearranged. The sum over all permutations of the product of
n
contractions contains every possible pair of contracted fields
\phi_{a}
and
\phi_{b}
with their momenta labelled respectively as
p_{a},p_{b}\in\{p_{1},\dots,p_{2n}\}
, where
a\neq b
. Although contraction is invariant under permutation between contracted fields, they are labelled by permutations which do care about the order. Therefore we fix the permutation so that
\sigma(2k_{0}-1)=a
and
\sigma(2k_{0})=b
, for some
k_{0}\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, and write (
4.7
) as

\tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2n})=\frac{1}{n!\,2^{n}}\ \sum_{\sigma\in S_{2n}}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{{\mathrm{i}}}{2}\,\sum\limits_{i<j}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{j}}\ \mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 18.16602pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=32.99173pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 18.16602pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=32.99173pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 15.87105pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=28.86012pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 15.57108pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=28.26016pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{\sigma(2k_{0}-1)}\,\phi_{\sigma(2k_{0})}\ \prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1\\
k\neq k_{0}\end{subarray}}^{n}\,\mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 16.2146pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=29.0889pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 16.2146pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=29.0889pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 13.91963pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=24.95729pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 13.61966pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=24.35733pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{\sigma(2k-1)}\,\phi_{\sigma(2k)}\ .

We can identify permutations
\sigma\in S_{2n}
and
\tau\in S_{2n-2}
such that
\sigma(2k-1)=\tau(2l-1)
and
\sigma(2k)=\tau(2l)
for
k\in\{1,\dots,\widehat{k}_{0},\dots,n\}
and
l\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
. Whereas
\sigma\in S_{2n}
permutes fields with every possible momentum in
\{p_{1},\dots,p_{2n}\}
, the permutation
\tau\in S_{2n-2}
only permutes fields that do not carry either momentum
p_{a}
or
p_{b}
, i.e. it permutes fields with every momenta in
\{p_{1},\dots,\widehat{p}_{a},\dots,\widehat{p}_{b},\dots,p_{2n}\}
.

Summing over
\sigma\in S_{2n}
is then equivalent to summing over
\tau\in S_{2n-2}
and over all possible unordered pairs
a\neq b
, or twice the sum over ordered pairs
a<b
:

\tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2n})=\frac{1}{n!\,2^{n}}\ 2\times\,\sum_{a<b}\,\mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 5.398pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=10.3855pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 5.398pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=10.3855pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.59024pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.82774pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.29025pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.22778pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{a}\,\phi_{b}\ \sum_{\tau\in S_{2n-2}}\ {\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{{\mathrm{i}}}{2}\,\sum\limits_{i<j}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{j}}\ \prod_{l=1}^{n-1}\,\mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 14.81621pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=26.29211pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 14.81621pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=26.29211pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 12.85077pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=22.81955pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 12.55078pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=22.21959pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{\tau(2l-1)}\,\phi_{\tau(2l)}\ .

We recognize the last sum as part of
\tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n-2}(p_{1},\dots\widehat{p}_{a},\dots,\widehat{p}_{b},\dots,p_{2n})
according to (
4.12
). In order to recreate the whole correlation function
\tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n-2}(p_{1},\dots\widehat{p}_{a},\dots,\widehat{p}_{b},\dots,p_{2n})
, we have to work out the phase factor in the external momenta, keeping in mind the ordering
(p_{1},\dots,p_{a},\dots,p_{b},\dots,p_{2n})
.

We extract terms containing
p_{a}
or
p_{b}
from the sum over external momenta to get

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i<j}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{j}

\displaystyle=-p_{a}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}+\sum\limits_{i<a}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{a}+\sum\limits_{a<i}\,p_{a}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}+\sum\limits_{i<b}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}+\sum\limits_{b<i}\,p_{b}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}

\displaystyle\qquad\,+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i^{\prime}<j^{\prime}\\
i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,\widehat{a},\dots,\widehat{b},\dots,n\}\end{subarray}}\,p_{i^{\prime}}\cdot\theta\,p_{j^{\prime}}\ .

The first term had to be subtracted since the second and third sums both contain the same term
p_{a}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}
. This yields

\displaystyle\tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2n})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\ \sum_{a<b}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{{\mathrm{i}}}{2}\,(-p_{a}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}+\sum\limits_{i<a}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{a}+\sum\limits_{a<i}\,p_{a}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}+\sum\limits_{i<b}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}+\sum\limits_{b<i}\,p_{b}\cdot\theta\,p_{i})}\ \mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 5.398pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=10.3855pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 5.398pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=10.3855pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.59024pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.82774pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.29025pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.22778pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{a}\,\phi_{b}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\times\frac{1}{(n-1)!\,2^{n-1}}\ \sum_{\tau\in S_{2n-2}}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{{\mathrm{i}}}{2}\,\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}<j^{\prime}}\,p_{i^{\prime}}\cdot\theta\,p_{j^{\prime}}}\ \prod_{l=1}^{n-1}\,\mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 14.81621pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=26.29211pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 14.81621pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=26.29211pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 12.85077pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=22.81955pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 12.55078pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=22.21959pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{\tau(2l-1)}\,\phi_{\tau(2l)}\ .

In the second line we identify the correlation function
\tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n-2}(p_{1},\dots\widehat{p}_{a},\dots,\widehat{p}_{b},\dots,p_{2n})
.

Whenever we contract fields carrying momenta
p_{a}
and
p_{b}
, the result contains the conserving delta-distribution
\delta(p_{a}+p_{b})
. Substituting
p_{a}=-p_{b}
in the remaining terms from the initial sum over external momenta we get

\displaystyle-p_{a}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}+\sum\limits_{i<a}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{a}+\sum\limits_{a<i}\,p_{a}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}+\sum\limits_{i<b}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}+\sum\limits_{b<i}\,p_{b}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum\limits_{i<a}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{a}+\sum\limits_{a<i<b}\,p_{a}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}+\sum\limits_{b<i}\,p_{a}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}+\sum\limits_{i<a}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}+\sum\limits_{a<i<b}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}+\sum\limits_{b<i}\,p_{b}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=-\sum\limits_{i<a}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}-\sum\limits_{a<i<b}\,p_{b}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}-\sum\limits_{b<i}\,p_{b}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}+\sum\limits_{i<a}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}+\sum\limits_{a<i<b}\,p_{i}\cdot\theta\,p_{b}+\sum\limits_{b<i}\,p_{b}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=-2\,\sum\limits_{a<i<b}\,p_{b}\cdot\theta\,p_{i}=-2\,p_{b}\cdot\theta\,(p_{a+1}+\dots+p_{b-1})\ .

Putting everything together, we arrive at the recursion relation

\tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2n})=\frac{1}{n}\ \sum_{a<b}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{\,{\mathrm{i}}\,p_{b}\cdot\theta(p_{a+1}+\dots+p_{b-1})}\ \mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 5.398pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=10.3855pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 5.398pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=10.3855pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.59024pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.82774pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.29025pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.22778pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{a}\,\phi_{b}\ \tilde{G}^{\star 0}_{2n-2}(p_{1},\dots,\widehat{p}_{a},\dots,\widehat{p}_{b},\dots,p_{2n})\ .

Since the order of the fields is immaterial, this is the same as the Schwinger–Dyson equation (
4.7
), which completes the proof of the braided Wick theorem (
4.12
).

4.3
Interacting theory

Consider now the deformation to the quantum differential which defines the interacting quantum field theory. It is given as usual by the small perturbation

\displaystyle{\bm{\delta}}=-\mathrm{i}\,\hbar\,\mathsf{\Delta}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}\,-\left\{\mathcal{S}_{\rm int},-\right\}\ .

In this case applying the recursion (
4.2
) to (
4.3
) yields

\tilde{G}_{n}^{\star}(p_{1},\dots,p_{n})=\tilde{\mathsf{P}}\,(-\mathrm{i}\,\hbar\,\mathsf{\Delta}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}\,\mathsf{H})\,(\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{n}})-\mathsf{\tilde{P}}\,\{\mathcal{S}_{\rm int},\mathsf{H}\,(\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{n}})\}_{\star}\ ,

(4.13)

for
n\geqslant 1
.

For the term involving the BV differential, we adapt our derivation of (
4.7
) to the interacting theory. Using the obvious analogue of (
4.5
) for
n
field insertions, and applying
\tilde{\mathsf{P}}=\mathsf{P}+\mathsf{P}_{{\bm{\delta}}}
to the remaining products of antifields, the first term on the right-hand side of (
4.13
) expands into

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{P}}\,(-\mathrm{i}\,\hbar\,\mathsf{\Delta}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}\,\mathsf{H})\,(\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{n}})=\frac{1}{n}\,\sum_{i\neq j}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{\,{\mathrm{i}}\,p_{j}\cdot\theta\,(p_{i+1}+\cdots+p_{j})}\ \mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.98808pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.26018pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.68811pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=7.66022pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{i}\,\phi_{j}\ \tilde{G}^{\star}_{n-2}(p_{1},\dots,\widehat{p_{i}},\dots,\widehat{p_{j}},\dots,p_{n})

for
n\geqslant 2
, where again we define
\tilde{G}_{0}^{\star}:=1
.

For the term involving the interaction functional, we observe that the operator
\{\mathcal{S}_{\rm int},-\}_{\star}\,\mathsf{H}
acts as strict derivation, due to the momentum conserving delta-functions in (
2.16
) which trivialise any braiding phase factors, and the antibrackets can only pair fields with antifields. Hence

\begin{split}\left\{\mathcal{S}_{\rm int},\mathsf{H}\,(\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\ldots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{n}})\right\}_{\star}&=\frac{1}{n}\,\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\mathtt{e}^{p_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\left\{\mathcal{S}_{\rm int},\mathsf{h}(\mathtt{e}^{p_{i}})\right\}_{\star}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{p_{n}}\ .\end{split}

Substituting the explicit form of the interaction functional (
2.15
), the remaining antibrackets can be evaluated using the braided derivation property to get

\displaystyle\big\{{\sf h}(\mathtt{e}^{p_{i}}),\mathtt{e}^{k_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{k_{r}}\big\}_{\star}=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}\,p_{i}\cdot(k_{1}+\cdots+k_{j-1})}\ \langle{\sf h}(\mathtt{e}^{p_{i}}),\mathtt{e}^{k_{j}}\rangle_{\star}\ \mathtt{e}^{k_{1}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\widehat{\mathtt{e}^{k_{j}}}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathtt{e}^{k_{r}}\ ,

for
r\geqslant 3
, where
k_{0}:=0
and the inner products are given by

\displaystyle\langle{\sf h}(\mathtt{e}^{p_{i}}),\mathtt{e}^{k_{j}}\rangle_{\star}=-\frac{(2\pi)^{d}\,\delta(k_{j}+p_{i})}{p_{i}^{2}-m^{2}}\ .

By relabelling the internal momenta
k_{j}
and using the braided symmetry of the vertices (
2.17
), we can eliminate all noncommutative phase factors and render each term in the sum over
j
equal to the term with
j=r
.

Applying the projection
\mathsf{\tilde{P}}
, the recursion relation (
4.13
) takes the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{G}_{n}^{\star}(p_{1},\dots,p_{n})&=\frac{1}{n}\,\sum_{i\neq j}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{\,{\mathrm{i}}\,p_{j}\cdot\theta\,(p_{i+1}+\cdots+p_{j-1})}\ \mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.64381pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=9.72977pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.98808pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.26018pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.68811pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=7.66022pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{i}\,\phi_{j}\ \tilde{G}^{\star}_{n-2}(p_{1},\dots,\widehat{p_{i}},\dots,\widehat{p_{j}},\dots,p_{n})\\
&\qquad\,+\sum_{r\geqslant 3}\,\frac{r}{n}\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\frac{1}{p_{i}^{2}-m^{2}}\ \int_{k_{1},\dots,k_{r-1}}\,V_{r}(k_{1},\dots,k_{r-1},-p_{i})\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\times\tilde{G}_{n+r-2}^{\star}(p_{1},\dots,p_{i-1},k_{1},\dots,k_{r-1},p_{i+1},\dots,p_{n})\ ,\end{split}

(4.14)

for
n\geqslant 1
, where we define
\tilde{G}_{-1}^{\star}:=0
, and the contraction of scalar fields in the first line is the free propagator (
4.6
).
This is our final form of the Schwinger–Dyson equations for a general braided scalar field theory. The expression (
4.14
) determines the full
n
-point function recursively in terms of correlation functions of both lower multiplicity
n-2
and higher multiplicities
n+r-2
for
r\geqslant 3
.

which unravels into the analytic expression

\displaystyle\tilde{G}_{2}^{\star}(p_{1},p_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 5.22223pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=10.44447pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 5.22223pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=10.44447pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.35558pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.71117pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}{\lower 2.15277pt\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.0556pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt\lower 3.80551pt\hbox{\vrule width=8.1112pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt}\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=4.30554pt}\hss}}}\phi_{1}\,\phi_{2}+\frac{\lambda}{4}\,\int_{k}\ \frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{\,\frac{{\mathrm{i}}}{2}\,k\cdot\theta\,p_{1}}}{p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}}\ \tilde{G}_{3}^{\star}(k,p_{1}-k,p_{2})+\frac{\lambda}{4}\,\int_{k}\ \frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{\,\frac{{\mathrm{i}}}{2}\,k\cdot\theta\,p_{2}}}{p_{2}^{2}-m^{2}}\ \tilde{G}_{3}^{\star}(p_{1},k,p_{2}-k)\ .

(4.16)

Thus the full propagator is determined recursively from the three-point vertex.
After multiplying (
4.16
) by
p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}=p_{2}^{2}-m^{2}
, the first term is identified as the contact term and the commutative limit
\theta=0
is the usual Schwinger–Dyson equation for
\lambda\,\phi^{3}
-theory.

5
Discussion and outlook

In this paper we studied the simplest class of examples of a braided noncommutative field theory: a real massive scalar field
\phi
in
d
dimensions with polynomial interactions and the Moyal–Weyl twist deformation. We used the framework of braided
L_{\infty}
-algebras and homological perturbation theory.

We studied the example of braided
\lambda\,\phi^{3}
-theory in detail. We derived explicit expressions for correlation functions up to two-loops and multiplicity three. Similarly to the commutative theory, the vacuum expectation value of the field
\phi
is different from zero. This manifests itself in a non-zero divergent tadpole diagram. We removed the tadpole diagram by extension to the effective theory based on a curved
L_{\infty}
-algebra, with a suitable central curvature that retains an interacting theory based on the same cochain complex of free fields as the flat case.

In addition to computational techniques of homotopical algebra, a novel graphical method for calculating correlation functions in homological perturbation theory was presented. This method significantly simplifies long calculations in higher order correlation functions and at higher loops. Both the analytic and diagrammatic methods lead to the same results: The two-point function up to two-loop order remains equal the corresponding commutative two-point function, while the three-point function has a noncommutative correction in the form of a phase factor in external momenta. From these results we conclude that, up to two-loops and multiplicity three, no UV/IR-mixing is present in braided
\lambda\,\phi^{3}
-theory. This is consistent with results reported in
[
30
,
31
,
32
,
33
]
through somewhat adhoc and less systematic computations than our rigorous formalism, but different from the standard noncommutative scalar field theory based on Feynman perturbation theory
[
27
,
2
]
.

We derived an algebraic version of the Schwinger–Dyson equations in (
4.14
) for arbitrary braided scalar field theories. Noncommutative corrections again appear as phase factors in the corresponding external momenta. Unlike the standard commutative Schwinger–Dyson equations that follow from the path integral formalism and single out a specific field insertion, the braided
L_{\infty}
-algebra formulation averages the usual Schwinger–Dyson equations over all permutations of the field insertions, leaving an expression that is manifestly braided symmetric. In the case of a free scalar field theory, we used the braided Schwinger–Dyson equations to give a rigorous proof of the braided Wick theorem proposed in
[
25
]
.

From the results obtained in the present paper we can conclude that the simple model of braided
\lambda\,\phi^{3}
-theory, up to two-loop order and multiplicity three, is free from UV/IR-mixing; this was also confirmed for the one-loop two-point function for braided
\phi^{4}
-theory in
[
25
]
. Nevertheless, the correlation functions of braided scalar field theories satisfy a braided noncommutative deformation of the usual Schwinger–Dyson equations. Our works
[
25
,
34
]
suggest that braided abelian gauge theories are also free from UV/IR-mixing. We plan to extend our work to braided non-abelian gauge theories and investigate their correlation functions as well as UV/IR-mixing.

In addition to correlation functions, it is also very important to study scattering amplitudes for a given braided field theory, as these encode the physical processes in the model. We will report in the future on the construction of amplitudes in braided field theories by using homological perturbation theory to develop a braided version of the perturbiner expansion. Quantum perturbiner expansions for both scattering amplitudes and correlation functions at arbitrary loop order are defined in
[
35
]
using Schwinger–Dyson equations. Given the availability of a braided version of the latter, as demonstrated in the present paper, this could prove to be a promising direction for constructing recursion relations for loop-level amplitudes and correlators of braided quantum field theories.

Appendix A
Braided Batalin–Vilkovisky quantization

In this appendix we briefly discuss braided
L_{\infty}
-algebras of field theory and quantization via the procedure of homotopy transfer. Further details can be found in
[
24
,
21
,
22
,
25
]
. Here we generalize the treatment to include curving, adapting the analogous formalism for curved
A_{\infty}
-algebras from
[
12
,
16
]
. We recommend the review
[
36
]
for the relevant mathematical background on the classical theory, which we extend to the braided setting in this paper.

Throughout this paper, degree shifting of a
\mathbbm{Z}
-graded vector space
V=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbbm{Z}}\,V_{k}
is performed under the convention
(V[l])_{k}=V_{k+l}
.
Consequently, linear maps of degree
l
between graded vector spaces
V
and
W
are written as
V\longrightarrow W[l]
, unless specified otherwise.
We assume the Einstein summation convention over repeated upper and lower indices.

A.1
Flat and curved braided
L_{\infty}
-algebras

Homotopy Lie algebras are vast generalisations of differential graded Lie algebras, where the antisymmetry of the binary bracket operation holds strictly, while the Jacobi identity only holds weakly, that is, up to coherent homotopies of higher operations. A braided homotopy Lie algebra is a homotopy Lie algebra in a symmetric monoidal category with a non-trivial braiding isomorphism, rather than the usual category of vector spaces which has trivial braiding. In this paper we work in the representation category of a triangular Hopf algebra where our braiding appears concretely through an associated
{\mathcal{R}}
-matrix via the Drinfel’d twist formalism, as discussed at the beginning of Section
2
. However, the discussion which follows also encompasses more general cases which do not necessarily arise from deformations of classical structures. See
[
24
,
21
,
22
]
for further details.

A
braided
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(over
\mathbbm{R}
) is a
\mathbbm{Z}
-graded real vector space
V=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbbm{Z}}\,V_{k}
with a sequence of graded braided antisymmetric multilinear maps
\ell_{n}^{\star}
of degree
2-n
for
n\geqslant 0
, called
n
-brackets
:

\displaystyle\ell^{\star}_{n}:V^{\otimes n}\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\,V[2-n]\ ,\quad v_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes v_{n}\longmapsto\ell^{\star}_{n}(v_{1},\dots,v_{n})\ ,

\displaystyle\ell^{\star}_{n}(\dots,v,v^{\prime},\dots)

\displaystyle\,=\>-(-1)^{|v|\,|v^{\prime}|}\ \ell^{\star}_{n}\big(\dots,\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}(v^{\prime}),\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}(v),\ldots\big)\ ,

where
|v|
denotes the degree of a homogeneous element
v\in V
.

The
n
-brackets are required to fulfil
braided homotopy Jacobi identities

\mathcal{J}^{\star}_{n}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})=0
for all
n\geqslant 0
and
v_{i}\in V
, where

\begin{split}\mathcal{J}^{\star}_{n}:=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,(-1)^{i\,(n-i)}\ \ell_{n+1-i}^{\star}\,\circ\,\big(\ell_{i}^{\star}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{V}^{\,\otimes\,n-i}\big)\ \circ\ \sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(i;n)}\,\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma)\ \sigma_{\mathcal{R}}\ .\end{split}

(A.1)

The shuffle group
\mathrm{Sh}(i;n)\subset S_{n}
represents how two decks of cards of size
(i,n-i)
are shuffled together once, with
\mathrm{Sh}(0;n)
consisting solely of the identity. The permutation
\sigma_{\mathcal{R}}
acts by braided transposition, and the sign of a permutation
{\rm sgn}(\sigma)
is given by the Koszul convention, taking into account the gradings of the elements of
V
.

A braided
L_{\infty}
-algebra is
flat
if
\ell_{0}^{\star}=0
. Then the braided homotopy Jacobi identities have sums running over
i=1,\dots,n
, and the first three relations are given by

\displaystyle\underline{n=1}:\quad\ell^{\star}_{1}\big(\ell^{\star}_{1}(v)\big)=0\ ,

(A.2)

\displaystyle\underline{n=2}:\quad\ell^{\star}_{1}\big(\ell^{\star}_{2}(v_{1},v_{2})\big)=\ell^{\star}_{2}\big(\ell^{\star}_{1}(v_{1}),v_{2}\big)+(-1)^{|v_{1}|}\ \ell^{\star}_{2}\big(v_{1},\ell^{\star}_{1}(v_{2})\big)\ ,

(A.3)

\displaystyle\underline{n=3}:\quad\ell_{1}^{\star}\big(\ell^{\star}_{3}(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})\big)=-\ell^{\star}_{3}\big(\ell^{\star}_{1}(v_{1}),v_{2},v_{3}\big)-(-1)^{|v_{1}|}\ \ell^{\star}_{3}\big(v_{1},\ell^{\star}_{1}(v_{2}),v_{3}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-(-1)^{|v_{1}|+|v_{2}|}\ \ell^{\star}_{3}\big(v_{1},v_{2},\ell^{\star}_{1}(v_{3})\big)-\ell^{\star}_{2}\big(\ell^{\star}_{2}(v_{1},v_{2}),v_{3}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-(-1)^{(|v_{1}|+|v_{2}|)\,|v_{3}|}\ \ell^{\star}_{2}\big(\ell^{\star}_{2}(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\,\mathsf{R}_{\beta}(v_{3}),\mathsf{R}^{\beta}(v_{1})),\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}(v_{2})\big)

(A.4)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-(-1)^{(|v_{2}|+|v_{3}|)\,|v_{1}|}\ \ell^{\star}_{2}\big(\ell^{\star}_{2}(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}(v_{2}),\mathsf{R}_{\beta}(v_{3})),\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\,\mathsf{R}^{\beta}(v_{1})\big)\ .

In particular, a flat braided
L_{\infty}
-algebra has an underlying cochain complex
(V,\ell^{\star}_{1})
, and the
2
-bracket
\ell_{2}^{\star}:V\otimes V\longrightarrow V
is a cochain map.
We recognise the third relation as the usual braided graded Jacobi identity up to the coboundary of the
3
-bracket
\ell_{3}^{\star}
.

A braided
L_{\infty}
-algebra is
curved
if the lowest map
\ell_{0}^{\star}:V^{\otimes 0}\simeq\mathbbm{R}\longrightarrow V[2]
is non-zero.
By linearity this map is uniquely fixed by its image at
1\in\mathbbm{R}
, called the
curvature
.
The first three braided homotopy Jacobi identities are now given by

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\underline{n=0}:\quad\ell^{\star}_{1}\big(\ell^{\star}_{0}(1)\big)=0\ ,

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\underline{n=1}:\quad\ell_{1}^{\star}\big(\ell_{1}^{\star}(v)\big)=-\ell_{2}^{\star}\big(\ell_{0}^{\star}(1),v\big)\ ,

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\underline{n=2}:\quad\ell_{1}^{\star}\big(\ell^{\star}_{2}(v_{1},v_{2})\big)+\ell_{3}^{\star}(\ell^{\star}_{0}(1),v_{1},v_{2})=\ell^{\star}_{2}\big(\ell^{\star}_{1}(v_{1}),v_{2}\big)+(-1)^{|v_{1}|}\ \ell^{\star}_{2}\big(v_{1},\ell^{\star}_{1}(v_{2})\big)\ .

In particular, the curvature
\ell^{\star}_{0}(1)
is ‘constant’ in the sense that it is
\ell^{\star}_{1}
-closed. The curvature controls the deviation of the
1
-bracket
\ell_{1}^{\star}
from a differential and a strict graded derivation of the
2
-bracket
\ell_{2}^{\star}
.

A braided
L_{\infty}
-algebra is
cyclic
if it additionally comes with a graded braided symmetric and non-degenerate bilinear map
\langle-,-\rangle_{\star}:V\otimes V\longrightarrow\mathbbm{R}[-3]
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\langle v_{0}\,,\,\ell^{\star}_{n}(v_{1},\dots,v_{n-1},v_{n})\rangle_{\star}\\[4.0pt]
&\hskip 85.35826pt=\pm\ \langle\,\mathsf{R}_{\alpha_{0}}\,\mathsf{R}_{\alpha_{1}}\cdots\mathsf{R}_{\alpha_{n-1}}(v_{n})\,,\,\ell_{n}^{\star}(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha_{0}}(v_{0}),\mathsf{R}^{\alpha_{1}}(v_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{R}^{\alpha_{n-1}}(v_{n-1}))\,\rangle_{\star}\ ,\end{split}

(A.5)

for all
n\geqslant 1
and
v_{i}\in V
.
Here and in the following we write
\pm
for the Koszul signs arising from the gradings when permuting elements involved in all operations.

A.2
Batalin–Vilkovisky formalism

As discussed in
[
6
,
8
]
, the data of any classical perturbative field theory are completely encoded in a corresponding
L_{\infty}
-algebra, through its Maurer–Cartan theory. The underlying graded vector space
V=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbbm{Z}}\,V_{k}
contains the gauge and higher gauge parameters for
k\leqslant 0
,
V_{1}
contains the physical fields,
V_{2}
contains the equations of motion, while
V_{k}
for
k\geqslant 3
contain the second Noether and higher second Noether identities. Lagrangian field theories further involve a cyclic structure which dually pairs fields in degrees
k\leqslant 1
with their
antifields
in degrees
3-k\geqslant 2
.

From the data of a classical Lagrangian field theory, the Batalin–Vilkovisky (BV) formalism constructs the
derived
space of classical observables of the theory. Starting from a braided
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(V,\{\ell^{\star}_{n}\})
with a cyclic structure
\langle-,-\rangle_{\star}
of degree
-3
, this is the braided commutative algebra
({\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[1])^{*},\odot_{\star})
, viewed as the space of braided symmetric polynomials on the vector space
V[1]
. Using the non-degenerate canonical symplectic pairing, we identify the dual
V^{*}\simeq V[1]
and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[1])^{*}\simeq{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])\ .

This data is supplemented by the antibracket, which is the graded braided Poisson structure

\displaystyle\{-,-\}_{\star}:{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])\otimes{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])[1]\ ,

defined on generators
v_{1},v_{2}\in V[2]
by

\displaystyle\{v_{1},v_{2}\}_{\star}=\langle v_{1},v_{2}\rangle_{\star}\ ,

and extended as a graded braided derivation on
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])
in each of its slots; for example

\displaystyle\{a_{1},a_{2}\odot_{\star}a_{3}\}_{\star}=\{a_{1},a_{2}\}_{\star}\odot_{\star}a_{3}\pm\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}(a_{2})\odot_{\star}\{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}(a_{1}),a_{3}\}_{\star}\ ,

(A.6)

for
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}\in{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}V[2]
.

Finally, the BV formalism constructs a differential

Q_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}:\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{R}}V[2]\longrightarrow(\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{R}}V[2])[1]

which is a strict graded derivation of the antibracket
\{-,-\}_{\star}
, while at the same time a braided graded derivation of the braided graded commutative product
\odot_{\star}
. The cohomology of
Q_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}
in degree
0
simultaneously encodes the quotients of the space of physical fields by the equations of motion and by the action of gauge transformations. The derived space of classical observables is thus fully specified by the triple
\big({\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2]),Q_{\textrm{\tiny BV}},\{-,-\}_{\star}\big)
, which has the structure of a braided
P_{0}
-algebra
[
21
]
.

Moving between the descriptions in terms of braided
L_{\infty}
-algebras and braided
P_{0}
-algebras is facilitated with the introduction of
contracted coordinate functions

[
8
]

\xi:=\mathtt{e}^{k}\otimes\mathtt{e}_{k}\ \in\ {\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])\otimes V

of degree
1
, for a basis
\{\mathtt{e}_{k}\}\subset V
with corresponding dual basis
\{\mathtt{e}^{k}\}\subset V^{*}\simeq V[3]
satisfying
\langle\mathtt{e}^{k},\mathtt{e}_{l}\rangle_{\star}=\delta^{k}_{l}
for all
k,l
.
The braided
L_{\infty}
-structure on
V
naturally extends to the tensor product
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])\otimes V
through the extended brackets

\displaystyle\begin{split}\ell_{0}^{\star\,{\rm ext}}(1)&=1\otimes\ell_{0}^{\star}(1)\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\ell_{1}^{\star\,\rm ext}(a_{1}\otimes v_{1})&=\pm\,a_{1}\otimes\ell^{\star}_{1}(v_{1})\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\ell_{n}^{\star\,{\rm ext}}(a_{1}\otimes v_{1},\dots,a_{n}\otimes v_{n})&=\pm\,\big(a_{1}\odot_{\star}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha^{1}_{1}}(a_{2})\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha_{n-1}^{1}}\cdots\mathsf{R}_{\alpha_{1}^{n-1}}(a_{n})\big)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\otimes\,\ell_{n}^{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha_{n-1}^{1}}\cdots\mathsf{R}^{\alpha_{1}^{1}}(v_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{R}^{\alpha_{1}^{n-1}}(v_{n-1}),v_{n}\big)\ ,\end{split}

for all
n\geqslant 2
,
a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\in{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}V[2]
and
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\in V
.
Similarly, the cyclic structure is extended via the
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}V[2]
-valued pairing

\displaystyle\langle a_{1}\otimes v_{1},a_{2}\otimes v_{2}\rangle^{\,\rm ext}_{\star}=\pm\,\big(a_{1}\odot_{\star}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}(a_{2})\big)\,\langle\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}(v_{1}),v_{2}\rangle_{\star}\ .

The BV functional is obtained from the contracted coordinate functions as the analogue of the curved braided Maurer–Cartan functional

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}=\sum_{n\geqslant 0}\,\frac{(-1)^{n\choose 2}}{(n+1)!}\,\langle\xi,\ell_{n}^{\star\,{\rm ext}}(\xi^{\otimes n})\rangle_{\star}^{\rm ext}\ \in\ ({\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}V[2])_{0}\ ,

where
{0\choose 2}:=0
.
Using the antibracket, this generates the BV differential on the space of observables as

\displaystyle Q_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}=\{\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}},-\}_{\star}\ ,

whose nilpotency
(Q_{\textrm{\tiny BV}})^{2}=0
is equivalent to the classical master equation

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}},\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}\}_{\star}=0\ .

This is equivalent to the braided homotopy Jacobi identities of the cyclic braided
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(V,\{\ell_{n}^{\star}\},\langle-,-\rangle_{\star})
, while compatibility with the antibracket
\{-,-\}_{\star}
is a consequence of cyclicity of the inner product
\langle-,-\rangle_{\star}
.

A.3
Homological perturbation lemma

Let us now explain how quantisation works in the framework of the BV formalism. If the curvature
\ell_{0}^{\star}(1)
is non-zero, then
\ell_{1}^{\star}
is not a differential in general, unless
\ell_{0}^{\star}(1)
is
central
:

\displaystyle\ell_{n+1}^{\star}\big(\ell_{0}^{\star}(1),v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\big)=0\ ,

for all
n\geqslant 1
and
v_{i}\in V
. This is a necessary and sufficient condition guaranteeing
(\ell_{1}^{\star})^{2}=0
. In this case we may study the field theory as a perturbation, which includes the constant curvature, around the solutions to its linearized equations of motion.

The underlying cochain complex
(V,\ell_{1}^{\star})
of the classical theory is homotopy equivalent to its minimal model
(H^{\bullet}(V),0)
through a diagram

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\ .

(A.7)

A homotopy equivalence is a set of maps
\mathsf{h}:V\longrightarrow V[-1]
such that the degree
0
cochain maps
\mathsf{p}:V\longrightarrow H^{\bullet}(V)
and
\iota:H^{\bullet}(V)\longrightarrow V
decompose the endomorphism
\iota\circ\mathsf{p}
into a Hodge–Kodaira relation

\iota\circ\mathsf{p}=\mathrm{id}_{{V}}+\ell^{\star}_{1}\circ\mathsf{h}+\mathsf{h}\circ\ell^{\star}_{1}\ .

(A.8)

This can always be refined to an increasingly stronger set of conditions:

•

{\sf p}\circ\iota=\mathrm{id}_{H^{\bullet}(V)}
, making the data a deformation retract.

•

{\sf h}^{2}=0
,
{\sf h}\circ\iota=0
and
{\sf p}\circ{\sf h}=0
, making the data a strong deformation retract.

In the Drinfel’d twist formalism, the kinetic term is unchanged in the braided theory, and so the cochain complexes of the deformed and undeformed theories are isomorphic.
In other words, the braided and unbraided free field theories are classically equivalent.

The strong deformation retract structure extends to the braided
P_{0}
-algebra of classical observables
\big({\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}V[2],\ell^{\star}_{1},\{-,-\}_{\star}\big)
of the
free
theory, where here
\ell_{1}^{\star}
is the linear part of the BV differential
Q_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}
extended from the differential of the
L_{\infty}
-structure as a strict graded derivation of symmetric degree
0
.
Using the symmetric tensor trick (reviewed in e.g.
[
29
]
), we obtain a strong deformation retract on the space of free classical observables.
This leads to the diagram

(A.9)

where the maps
\mathsf{P}
,
\mathsf{I}
and
\mathsf{H}
are extensions of the corresponding maps in (
A.7
) to braided symmetric algebras using the symmetric tensor trick.

To incorporate interactions and quantize the theory, we use the homological perturbation lemma, see e.g.
[
37
,
7
]
, adapted to the braided setting in
[
21
]
.
The differential
\ell_{1}^{\star}
is perturbed to the differential
Q_{{\bm{\delta}}}=\ell_{1}^{\star}+{\bm{\delta}}
, where
{\bm{\delta}}
is a small perturbation in the sense that the map
\mathrm{id}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])}-{\bm{\delta}}\,\mathsf{H}
is invertible.
This leads to a new strong deformation retract with new maps
\tilde{\mathsf{P}}
,
\tilde{\mathsf{I}}
and
\tilde{\mathsf{H}}
fitting into the diagram

(A.10)

In this paper we only need the explicit form of the deformed projection map, which is given by
\tilde{\mathsf{P}}=\mathsf{P}+\mathsf{P}_{{\bm{\delta}}}
with

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{{\bm{\delta}}}=\mathsf{P}\,\big(\mathrm{id}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])}-{{\bm{\delta}}}\,\mathsf{H}\big)^{-1}\,{\bm{\delta}}\,\mathsf{H}\ .

(A.11)

In the classical setting, it was shown in
[
7
]
that
\mathsf{P}_{{\bm{\delta}}}
computes the path integral.

A.4
Braided Laplacian

The free braided quantum field theory is defined by the perturbation
{\bm{\delta}}_{0}=-{\mathrm{i}}\,\hbar\,{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}
with the BV Laplacian

{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}:{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])[1]\ .

The BV Laplacian satisfies the two key properties
({\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}})^{2}=0
and
{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}\circ\ell_{1}^{\star}=-\ell_{1}^{\star}\circ{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}
which guarantee that
Q_{{\bm{\delta}}_{0}}=\ell^{\star}_{1}-{\mathrm{i}}\,\hbar\,{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}
is a differential,
(Q_{{\bm{\delta}}_{0}})^{2}=0
.
Its failure to derive the braided graded commutative product is measured by the antibracket through

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}(a_{1}\odot_{\star}a_{2})={\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}(a_{1})\odot_{\star}a_{2}+(-1)^{|a_{1}|}\,a_{1}\odot_{\star}{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}(a_{2})+\{a_{1},a_{2}\}_{\star}\ ,

(A.12)

for all
a_{1},a_{2}\in{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathcal{R}}(V[2])
. Equivalently,
{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}
is a strict graded derivation of the antibracket.

Explicitly, denoting the identity by
1\in(\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{R}}V[2])_{0}
, the BV Laplacian is defined by its images on generators through

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}(1)=0

\displaystyle\qquad,\qquad{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}(v_{1})=0\qquad,\qquad{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}(v_{1}\odot_{\star}v_{2})=\langle v_{1},v_{2}\rangle_{\star}\ ,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}(v_{1}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}v_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i<j}\,\pm\,\langle v_{i},\mathsf{R}_{\alpha_{i+1}}\cdots\mathsf{R}_{\alpha_{j-1}}(v_{j})\rangle_{\star}\ v_{1}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}v_{i-1}

(A.13)

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt\odot_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha_{i+1}}(v_{i+1})\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha_{j-1}}(v_{j-1})\odot_{\star}v_{j+1}\odot_{\star}\cdots\odot_{\star}v_{n}\ ,

for all
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\in V[2]
.

The interacting braided quantum field theory is defined by taking
{\bm{\delta}}=-{\mathrm{i}}\,\hbar\,{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}-\{\mathcal{S}_{\rm int},-\}_{\star}
, where
\mathcal{S}_{\rm int}
consists of the curved and interacting parts of the BV functional
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\rm int}=\langle\xi,\ell_{0}^{\star\,{\rm ext}}(1)\rangle_{\star}^{\rm ext}+\sum_{n\geqslant 2}\,\frac{(-1)^{n\choose 2}}{(n+1)!}\,\langle\xi,\ell_{n}^{\star\,{\rm ext}}(\xi^{\otimes n})\rangle_{\star}^{\rm ext}\ .

The classical master equation together with
{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}}(\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{\tiny BV}})=0
ensure that
Q_{{\bm{\delta}}}
is a differential.
Then (
A.11
) is a formal power series in
\hbar
and in the coupling constants appearing in
\mathcal{S}_{\rm int}
, representing the perturbative expansion.

Appendix B
Glossary of diagrams

In this appendix we present the analytic expressions for the diagrams that appear in the loop calculations of Section
3
for braided
\lambda\,\phi^{3}
-theory using our graphical calculus. The explicit formulas up to two loops and multiplicity three are given as follows.
